# The Tide Mother — Image Prompts for Grok Imagine (short clips, one image per clip)

*424 beats, 31:02 total. Every beat is **3–6 seconds** (only the narrated prologue beats run 10–15 s) and starts from **its own image**, so faces, costumes and voices never drift. Conversations are shot **from behind the listener**: the listener's back is in the foreground and the speaker faces the camera. Nights are truly dark, lit only by four fixed medieval light sources. Visuals follow the original FABLEON Visual Style Profile.*

**How to use:** generate every beat's image with the character sheets and the location sheet attached as references. Never chain beats from the last frame of the previous video; always start each beat from its own image.


---

## PROLOGUE — THE LAW OF KELVHAVEN


### Prologue — Kelvhaven


**Beat 001 · 0:00–0:06 · 6s · Opening hook**

- **Script segment:** "Black water, only drifting particles. A faint blue glow pulses somewhere far below. A huge luminous violet eye snaps open and stares straight into the lens."
- **Season:** Start of winter (the great storm): cold rain, wind and sea spray; no snow
- **Location sheet:** none (open sea or underwater)
- **Image prompt:** Photorealistic cinematic film still, dark fantasy, shot on 35mm, fine film grain, high-fidelity textures, shallow depth of field, natural bokeh, desaturated high-contrast color grade, 16:9. Extreme close-up, underwater: a single huge luminous violet slit-pupil eye opening in pitch-black water, faint faint violet veins around it. The moment in this clip: Black water, only drifting particles. Setting: deep underwater in a cold northern sea, drifting particles, kelp shadows, light fading from the surface far above. Lighting: darkness pierced by soft violet glow. Mood: ominous, mysterious. Palette: storm slate grey, fog grey, driftwood brown and wet-wool grey. No text, no watermark, no modern objects.

**Beat 002 · 0:06–0:21 · 15s · Dialogue and character emotion**

- **Script segment:** "NARRATOR: At the very edge of the northern sea sits a village most maps forget. Its name is Kelvhaven. And it lives by one strange rule."
- **Season:** Start of winter (the great storm): cold rain, wind and sea spray; no snow
- **Location sheet:** L0 Kelvhaven master aerial
- **Image prompt:** Photorealistic cinematic film still, dark fantasy, shot on 35mm, fine film grain, high-fidelity textures, shallow depth of field, natural bokeh, desaturated high-contrast color grade, 16:9. Aerial wide shot: a sweeping drone view skimming the grey northern sea at dusk toward black cliffs and a small fishing village on the bay. Setting: the northern sea at dusk under heavy grey cloud, black sea cliffs ahead and a small fishing village of grey fieldstone cottages with turf roofs on the bay. Lighting: the last cold light of dusk through heavy grey cloud, soft and diffused. Mood: mythic, mysterious, ominous. Palette: storm slate grey, fog grey, driftwood brown and wet-wool grey. No text, no watermark, no modern objects.

**Beat 003 · 0:21–0:36 · 15s · Dialogue and character emotion**

- **Script segment:** "NARRATOR: Three hundred years ago, its first families crawled ashore from a shipwreck with nothing but their nets. They built their homes from the stones the tide gave them."
- **Season:** Start of winter (the great storm): cold rain, wind and sea spray; no snow
- **Location sheet:** L0 Kelvhaven master aerial
- **Image prompt:** Photorealistic cinematic film still, dark fantasy, shot on 35mm, fine film grain, high-fidelity textures, shallow depth of field, natural bokeh, desaturated high-contrast color grade, 16:9. Aerial shot, high angle: a timber harbor with clinker-built rowboats below grey fieldstone cottages with turf roofs. Setting: a cold northern fishing village seen from above, wind-bent grey fieldstone cottages with turf roofs, a timber harbor with clinker-built rowboats, fish drying racks, lobster pots and hanging nets. Lighting: flat diffused overcast storm light, soft and cold, sea spray in the air. Mood: mythic, mysterious, ominous. Characters: villagers in medieval northern fishing-village clothing all in black: black oiled-leather jerkins and black leather hooded cloaks over black wool tunics and kirtles, black leather belts, leg-wraps and leather turnshoes, among them the same recurring faces in every scene, all in black: Jory (about 18, lanky, messy blond hair, grey wool cap), Ulf (big man in his 30s, shaved head, iron gaff), Sef (wiry man in his 30s, black beard, crooked nose), Garth (about 60, bald, short grey beard), Brenna (fishwife in her 40s, red-brown braid) and Mags (about 70, white hair); everyone else keeps their hood up, faces in shadow; people of different heights and builds. The villagers are people of different heights and builds, each in a different natural pose, their lights held at uneven, random heights. Nobody looks into the camera lens. Palette: storm slate grey, fog grey, driftwood brown and wet-wool grey. No text, no watermark, no modern objects.

Lighting: flat diffused overcast storm light, soft and cold, sea spray in the air. Mood: mythic, mysterious, ominous. **Beat 005 · 0:46–1:01 · 15s · Dialogue and character emotion**

- **Script segment:** "NARRATOR: Only one law survived. Whatever the sea gives, the sea may take back. And anyone who hides the sea's gift is given back to it."
- **Season:** Start of winter (the great storm): cold rain, wind and sea spray; no snow
- **Location sheet:** L10 The cliff edge and the drowning stone
- **Image prompt:** Photorealistic cinematic film still, dark fantasy, shot on 35mm, fine film grain, high-fidelity textures, shallow depth of field, natural bokeh, desaturated high-contrast color grade, 16:9. Wide shot: an ancient stone with deep rope grooves at the edge of a high black sea cliff, the sea crashing far below. Setting: the edge of a high black basalt sea cliff, gale wind, the churning sea far below. Lighting: flat diffused overcast storm light, soft and cold, sea spray in the air. Mood: ominous. Palette: storm slate grey, fog grey, driftwood brown and wet-wool grey. No text, no watermark, no modern objects.

**Beat 006 · 1:01–1:11 · 10s · Dialogue and character emotion**

- **Script segment:** "NARRATOR: For three hundred years, nobody broke that law. Not once. Then came the winter of the great storm."
- **Season:** Start of winter (the great storm): cold rain, wind and sea spray; no snow
- **Location sheet:** L3 The main lane
- **Image prompt:** Photorealistic cinematic film still, dark fantasy, shot on 35mm, fine film grain, high-fidelity textures, shallow depth of field, natural bokeh, desaturated high-contrast color grade, 16:9. Aerial wide shot, night: the fishing village asleep under a moonlit sky, one cottage window glowing with candlelight. Setting: a cold northern fishing village at night, grey fieldstone cottages with turf roofs, one small window glowing with candlelight, the dark sea beyond. Lighting: cold moonlight through drifting cloud, deep shadows, one warm candle-lit window. TRUE NIGHT: deep darkness and a black sky; faces are lit only by the nearby flame or glow, and everything outside its reach falls into black; no daylight anywhere. Mood: foreboding. Palette: storm slate grey, fog grey, driftwood brown and wet-wool grey. No text, no watermark, no modern objects.

**Beat 007 · 1:11–1:26 · 15s · Dialogue and character emotion**

- **Script segment:** "NARRATOR: That night, a young widow named Maren walked down to the sea alone. What she carried back would break that law, split the village in two, and wake something that had been sleeping for a very long time."
- **Season:** Start of winter (the great storm): cold rain, wind and sea spray; no snow
- **Location sheet:** L3 The main lane
- **Image prompt:** Photorealistic cinematic film still, dark fantasy, shot on 35mm, fine film grain, high-fidelity textures, shallow depth of field, natural bokeh, desaturated high-contrast color grade, 16:9. Medium shot: Maren, heavily pregnant, stepping out of her cottage door into the storm with a horn lantern. Setting: the edge of a northern fishing village at night as a storm rolls in from the sea, rain lashing the stone cottages. Lighting: lightning flashes and a single horn lantern, deep black shadows, cold blue-grey rim light. TRUE NIGHT: deep darkness and a black sky; faces are lit only by the nearby flame or glow, and everything outside its reach falls into black; no daylight anywhere. Mood: suspense. Characters: Maren, a heavily pregnant woman about 30 with long wet tangled dark-brown hair with thin braids, pale weathered freckled skin, tired grey-green eyes, wearing a long black wool medieval dress with long sleeves and a full skirt to the ankles, a black wool hooded capelet (short shoulder cape) with stitched edges, fastened at the throat with a carved bone toggle, a braided brown rope belt with a long iron net-mending needle hanging from it, dark wool leg-wraps and worn brown leather turnshoes, and a moss-green knitted shawl around her shoulders. Nobody looks into the camera lens. Palette: storm slate grey, fog grey, driftwood brown and wet-wool grey. No text, no watermark, no modern objects. Fixed medieval light sources, same design in every scene: the horn lantern is a small black iron-framed medieval lantern with scraped-horn panes and a tallow candle inside.

### Cave mouth — the mob


**Beat 252 · 18:47–18:52 · 5s · Major climax**

- **Script segment:** "A line of torches at a sea cave mouth at low tide, villagers pushing in, a horse-sized sea dragon rising behind a woman."
- **Season:** Early winter chill: cold wind, grey skies, drizzle and sea mist; no snow, no ice
- **Location sheet:** L11 The hidden cove
- **Image prompt:** Photorealistic cinematic film still, dark fantasy, shot on 35mm, fine film grain, desaturated high-contrast color grade, 16:9. REAR VIEW. High drone shot from behind and above a running mob, looking forward over their heads along a long dark black-sand beach toward a sea cave far away. This is a rear tracking shot: the camera is BEHIND the mob, so we see ONLY THE BACKS of the villagers. LOWER HALF OF THE FRAME (closest to the camera): about twenty-five villagers running AWAY from the camera, toward the top of the frame. We see the backs of their heads, raised hoods, the backs of their cloaks and coats, and the soles of their boots lifting as they run. No villager's face is visible. Their cloaks and the smoke of their pitch torches stream BACKWARD toward the camera from the speed of running. They hold the pitch torches raised high and forward, at uneven heights, so the orange light falls on the wet sand IN FRONT of them, and their backs are dark silhouettes rimmed with orange light. A long trail of fresh footprints in the wet sand runs from the bottom edge of the frame up to the mob. At the very front, the broadest back of all: a big man with curly red-brown hair in a heavy, shaggy brown and tan sheepskin fleece coat, an iron harpoon held up in his right hand. UPPER THIRD OF THE FRAME (about 70 meters away, small because of the distance): the dark mouth of a sea cave at the far end of the beach. In front of it stands a lone woman in a long black wool kirtle and black hooded mantle, FACING THE CAMERA and the oncoming mob, arms spread. Just behind her shoulder stands a horse-sized sea dragon, his head level with hers, blush-pink pearly scales, faint glowing violet eyes and violet veins. These two are the only faces in the image, small and far away. Between the mob and the cave: 70 meters of empty dark wet sand reflecting the torchlight. TRUE NIGHT: deep darkness, black sky, a thin moon behind cloud. The only light is the moving orange torchlight around the mob and the faint violet glow at the far cave. Early winter chill, drizzle, sea mist, no snow, no ice. Palette: storm slate grey, fog grey, driftwood brown and wet-wool grey, with orange pitch torches against cold blue darkness. No faces on the villagers, no one looking at the camera, no text, no watermark, no modern objects. Fixed medieval light sources, same design in every scene: pitch torches are wooden staves with rag heads soaked in fish oil and pitch.
